name: unslop
description: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.
---

# Unslop

Edit text to remove AI patterns and add human voice.

## Process

1. Scan for the patterns below.
2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.
3. Add soul (see next section).
4. Self-audit: "What makes this obviously AI generated?" Fix remaining tells.

## Adding soul

Removing patterns is half the job. Sterile, voiceless writing is just as obvious.

- **Have opinions.** React to facts instead of neutrally listing pros and cons.
- **Vary rhythm.** Short sentences. Then longer ones that take their time. Mix it up.
- **Acknowledge complexity.** "Impressive but also kind of unsettling" beats "impressive."
- **Use "I" when it fits.** First person isn't unprofessional.
- **Let some mess in.** Perfect structure looks machine-made.
- **Be specific.** Not "this is concerning" but "there's something unsettling about agents churning away at 3am."

## Patterns to detect and fix

### Content

1. **Puffery.** "pivotal moment", "testament to", "evolving landscape", "setting the stage for", "indelible mark", "deeply rooted". Cut puffery, state what happened.
2. **Name-dropping.** Listing media outlets without context. Pick one, say what was said.
3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** "highlighting...", "ensuring...", "reflecting...", "showcasing...", "fostering...". Delete or expand with real sources.
4. **Promotional language.** "nestled", "vibrant", "breathtaking", "groundbreaking", "renowned", "stunning", "must-visit". Use neutral descriptions.
5. **Vague attributions.** "Experts believe", "Industry reports suggest", "Some critics argue". Name the source or delete.
6. **Formulaic challenges.** "Despite challenges... continues to thrive." Replace with specific facts.

### Language

7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.
8. **Fancy ways to say "is".** "serves as", "stands as", "boasts", "features". Just say "is" or "has".
9. **Scripted negation.** "Not just X, but Y", "No X, no Y, no Z", "Didn't flinch, didn't blink", "Don't call it X, call it Y". State the claim directly once.
10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.
11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.
12. **False ranges.** "from X to Y" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.

### Style

13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). Em dashes are an AI tell, and reaching for parentheses instead just trades one tell for another. If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.
14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. "If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. "Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English." Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.
15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.
16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: "**Performance:** Performance improved...". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and adds new detail ("**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.") is fine, not a tell.
17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.
18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.
19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.

### Communication artifacts

20. **Chatbot phrases.** "I hope this helps", "Let me know if", "Would you like", "Of course", "Certainly", "Found the smoking gun", "One honest caveat", "Here's what gets me", "The key insight is", "The punchline is", "Sit with that", "You already know the answer". Delete the greeting, stage direction, or recap. State the point.
21. **Cutoff disclaimers.** "While specific details are limited..." Find sources or remove.
22. **Sycophantic tone.** "Great question! You're absolutely right!" Respond directly.
23. **Manufactured gravity.** "the real problem", "genuinely", "actually", "truly", "that's the whole point", "X is the entire game", "the improvement is real and not subtle", "that loss is worth naming", "that's not nothing". These insist something matters instead of showing why. Give the fact, consequence, or measurement. Keep an intensifier only when it marks a literal contrast.
24. **Anthropomorphized tools.** Code does not "want to live" somewhere, feel "happiest" there, or "earn its keep". Name the constraint: cost, speed, ownership, or repeatability.

### Filler

25. **Filler phrases.** "In order to" becomes "To". "Due to the fact that" becomes "Because". "It is important to note that" gets deleted.
26. **Excessive hedging.** "could potentially possibly be argued that it might" becomes "may".
27. **Generic conclusions.** "The future looks bright." State specific plans or facts.

### Jargon

28. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in "API surface"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. "Substrate" becomes "base". "Wedge in" becomes "add". "Vector" becomes "way" or "method". "Gold-plating" becomes "more than the job needs". "Ratchet" becomes the mechanism's real name or "a limit that only tightens". "Evacuate" becomes "move out". "Endgame" becomes "the last phase". Pick the concrete word.

### Plain speech

29. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** "the database stays close at hand", "SQL you can read", "types that follow your schema" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: "`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database", "a column rename fails the build". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.
30. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.
31. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch "is/are/was/were + past participle" and name the actor: "queries are validated" becomes "the compiler validates queries", "the file is parsed by the loader" becomes "the loader parses the file". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or doesn't matter.
32. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** "runs quickly" becomes "is fast" or the number. "significantly improves" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.
33. **Prefer the plain word.** "utilize" becomes "use", "leverage" becomes "use", "facilitate" becomes "help", "numerous" becomes "many", "in the event that" becomes "if". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.

name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
---

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
❌ Trust agent report
```

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
❌ Trust agent report
```

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

### Plain speech

29. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** "the database stays close at hand", "SQL you can read", "types that follow your schema" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: "`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database", "a column rename fails the build". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.
30. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.
31. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch "is/are/was/were + past participle" and name the actor: "queries are validated" becomes "the compiler validates queries", "the file is parsed by the loader" becomes "the loader parses the file". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or doesn't matter.
32. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** "runs quickly" becomes "is fast" or the number. "significantly improves" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.
33. **Prefer the plain word.** "utilize" becomes "use", "leverage" becomes "use", "facilitate" becomes "help", "numerous" becomes "many", "in the event that" becomes "if". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
❌ Trust agent report
```

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state

- Moving to next task

- Exact phrases

 succeeded in 0ms:
---
type: practice
title: Pin self-signed certificates by fingerprint and warn before plain HTTP
description: >-
  Trust a self-signed cert only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint, never accept
  any cert, and put plain HTTP behind a stronger warning.
tags:
  - security
  - network
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to:
  - map-api-client-shared-okhttp-serverurl-and-errors
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app connects to any Tokkl server, however it is set up. The Tokkl server itself listens on plain HTTP, and HTTPS comes from a reverse proxy or `tailscale serve` in front of it. Real servers use a public certificate, a certificate from `tailscale cert`, a self-signed certificate, or Tokkl's default of plain HTTP on port 8080.

HTTPS with a publicly trusted certificate or a CA the user installed in Android works (S2). For a self-signed certificate the user reads a disclaimer and trusts it. The app shows the certificate's SHA-256 fingerprint, trusts that certificate only, and asks again when it changes. It never accepts any certificate without a check (S3). The app pins the certificate itself, separate from Android's trust store.

Plain `http://` works only after a warning stronger than the self-signed one. The warning says the token travels unencrypted unless the network encrypts it, as Tailscale does (S4). Android 9 and later block cleartext unless the app opts in, and Android 7 and later trust only system CAs by default. So `res/xml/network_security_config.xml` permits cleartext and trusts system and user-added CAs. The self-signed pin stays out of that file and goes into the trust manager of `SharedHttpClient`, the app's only `OkHttpClient`.

[1] [docs/PRD.md](../../../docs/PRD.md)

---
type: practice
title: Match Tokkl web app behavior in the account timezone
description: >-
  Use the web app's time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo, in the account
  timezone read from GET /api/v9/me.
tags:
  - api
  - ui
  - time
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to:
  - practice-port-tokkl-typescript-with-javascript-semantics
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app uses the same time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo as Tokkl's web app, in the account's timezone. Moving between the laptop and the phone then needs no relearning.

Read the timezone, first day of the week and `time_resolution_minutes` from `GET /api/v9/me`, and use the account timezone everywhere (S7). Resolve its name with `time.parseTimeZone`, never `ZoneId.of`. Tokkl accepts what JavaScript's Intl accepts, such as `europe/madrid` in any case and ICU-only names like `IST`, and rejects some names `ZoneId.of` takes, such as `Z` or `GMT+5`. A name it cannot resolve gives null. Never fall back to the device's zone. Show stopped entries as the server returns them, after it snaps them to `time_resolution_minutes` (T3). The phone snaps entries it has not sent yet in the account timezone, so their times do not change when the server answers. History and queue code calls `snapUnsent` in `time/Snap.kt`, never `snapSpan` directly. The server cuts both ends to whole seconds before it snaps (`snapToResolution` in `../tokkl/packages/api/src/models/timeEntries.ts`), and `snapUnsent` does the same. With no `time_resolution_minutes` in `/me` it returns the span unchanged. Never pass it the running entry, whose clock is not snapped.

Manual entry accepts times like `9`, `930`, `9:30` and `4pm`, entries across midnight, and durations like `1h 30m`, `90` and `1,5h` (M1). The parsing is a Kotlin port of Tokkl's `parseDuration` in `../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts` and `parseTimeOfDay` in `../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/timeOfDay.ts`, checked against the cases in the `.test.ts` file next to each. The time-of-day parser lives in the web package, not in `packages/common`. Both ports live in `com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time`, and their tests copy the TypeScript cases verbatim at a recorded tokkl commit (see the porting practice node). The ports can still drift when the TypeScript changes later. Sharing the test cases between the two repos would catch that.

Favorites are the same ones the web app shows, read from and written to `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.

---
The HTTP code lives in `app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/api/`. `SharedHttpClient.instance` is the app's only `OkHttpClient`, so its connection pool, dispatcher and security settings apply to every request. New security settings, such as the trust manager for a pinned self-signed certificate, go into its `build()`, never into a second client. `SingleHttpClientTest` reads the constant pool of every compiled app class and fails when any class but `SharedHttpClient` builds an HTTP client, so an import alias or a fully qualified name cannot hide one.

`ConfiguredServerGuard`, an interceptor on that client, throws `HostNotAllowedException` for any request whose scheme, host or port differs from `ConfiguredServer.current`. It does so before OkHttp connects, so no credentials leave the phone, and `TogglApiClient` returns it as `ApiError.HostNotAllowed(origin)` with the origin as `scheme://host:port`. `current` is the stored server, or the URL being tried inside `ConfiguredServer.signingIn`. With neither, every request is refused. OkHttp's own redirects are off (`followRedirects(false)`, `followSslRedirects(false)`). The guard follows a redirect itself, and only to the same origin.

[credential-related line omitted]

`TogglApiClient.get` and `send` decode a 2xx body with a kotlinx.serialization deserializer, and an empty body fails to decode. Calls that expect no content use `sendNoContent`, which treats any 2xx as success and ignores the body. Tokkl's DELETE routes for time entries, tasks, clients and tags answer 200 with an empty body, while the project DELETE answers 200 with the project id as JSON (`../tokkl/packages/api/src/routes/togglV9.ts`). `sendNoContent` handles both.

Every call returns `ApiResult.Success` or `ApiResult.Failure(ApiError)`. 403 maps to `AuthFailed`, 429 to `RateLimited` with the `Retry-After` seconds (null when the header is missing or not a whole number), and any other status outside 2xx to `UnexpectedStatus`. `Network` wraps the `IOException` of a request that got no response. `InvalidResponse` is a 2xx body that is not the expected JSON.

---
type: map
title: 'Tokkl and Toggl API endpoints, auth and Tokkl-only extensions'
description: >-
  Tokkl speaks a subset of Toggl API v9 at /api/v9 and Reports v3 at
  /reports/api/v3 with Basic auth, plus Tokkl-only fields and endpoints.
tags:
  - api
  - toggl
  - network
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: map-tokkl-toggl-api
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
Tokkl implements a subset of the Toggl Track API v9 at `https://<server>/api/v9` and of the Reports API v3 at `https://<server>/reports/api/v3`. A client that accepts a base URL needs no other change to talk to it. The server URL may carry a path, such as `https://example.com/tokkl`. Toggl Track's API lives at `api.track.toggl.com`.

[credential-related line omitted]

Tokkl adds `role` and `time_resolution_minutes` to `GET /api/v9/me`, and the server snaps stopped entries to that resolution. Tokkl-only endpoints outside the Toggl API cover the trash (`GET/DELETE /api/app/trash`, `DELETE /api/app/trash/{id}`) and undoing a delete (`POST /api/app/time_entries/{id}/restore`). Favorites are a Tokkl key in `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.

The first version targets Tokkl only. Toggl Track comes later by pointing the app at Toggl's base URL, and that version hides the Tokkl-only features.

# Citations

[1] [docs/PRD.md](../../../docs/PRD.md)

---
type: practice
title: Talk only to the configured server and use only free software
description: >-
  No telemetry, analytics, crash reporting, ads or proprietary libraries. The
  app sends requests only to the configured server.
tags:
  - privacy
  - network
  - fdroid
  - security
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-talk-only-to-configured-server
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to:
  - map-api-client-shared-okhttp-serverurl-and-errors
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
Data stays between the phone and the server. The app has no telemetry, analytics, crash reporting or ads, and sends no request to any host other than the configured server. This carries Tokkl's first principle over and matches F-Droid's rules. `ConfiguredServerGuard` on the app's single OkHttp client refuses every other host, and `SingleHttpClientTest` fails when code builds a second client.

Every dependency must be free software. F-Droid builds the app from source with a free toolchain and rejects proprietary libraries such as Google Play Services and Firebase. So the app has no push through FCM.

`:app:checkDeniedDependencies` enforces this. It fails when the release runtime classpath holds a module from a group listed in `gradle/denied-dependency-groups.txt`, or from a group below one, and prints the path that pulled it in. Each line of that file reads `<group> # <reason>`. `check` depends on the task, and CI runs `./gradlew assembleDebug check`.

---

# Read timezone, week start and time resolution from /me and use the account timezone everywhere

## Problem

Without the account settings, the app shows times and week boundaries in the phone's timezone, and they disagree with the server and the web app. The PRD says to read the timezone, first day of the week and `time_resolution_minutes` from `GET /api/v9/me` and use the account timezone everywhere. Tokkl adds `time_resolution_minutes` to `/me`, and the server snaps stopped entries to it. Servers without the field have no snapping to show, so a missing field is not an error.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:52
- docs/PRD.md:94
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

- After sign-in and on every refresh, read `timezone`, `beginning_of_week` and the optional `time_resolution_minutes` from `GET /api/v9/me`.
- Keep them in one `AccountSettings` source that persists across restarts, so offline screens still have them.
- Provide one `java.time` zone and clock utility. Every screen uses it for display and input, and no screen reads the device zone directly.
- Treat a missing `time_resolution_minutes` as no snapping.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] An instrumented test reads the settings from the real Tokkl server.
- [ ] A JVM test with a device zone different from the account zone shows times in the account zone.
- [ ] A `/me` response without `time_resolution_minutes` causes no error.
- [ ] The settings persist across restarts for offline use.

# Warn before using a plain http:// server that the token travels unencrypted

## Problem

[credential-related line omitted]

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:55
- docs/PRD.md:91
- docs/PRD.md:197
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

- When the server URL scheme is `http`, show a warning before any request that carries credentials.
[credential-related line omitted]
- Tapping outside does not dismiss the warning. Continuing needs a separate explicit confirmation, a stronger step than the self-signed disclaimer asks for.
- The block must hold for every credentialed request, not only the sign-in button, so check it where requests are built or sent.
- Remember the confirmation per server URL. Ask again when the URL changes.
- `https` URLs never show the warning.
- Keep the text in English string resources and give every control a TalkBack label. Meet WCAG AA contrast, never use colour as the only signal, and keep it usable at the largest font scale.

## Acceptance criteria

id: TKA-0021
title: Trust a self-signed certificate only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint after a disclaimer
status: done
type: feature
milestone: server-connection
priority: p2
effort: l
wave: 5
created: 2026-10-17
updated: 2026-10-18
labels: [security, tls, network]
depends_on: [TKA-0016]
source: "sift-prime from docs/PRD.md, 2026-10-17"
resolution: "TKA-0021: PinningTrustManager delegates to the platform trust manager, then accepts only the leaf whose SHA-256 is pinned for that host and port, else UntrustedCertificateException routing to a prompt with fingerprint and subject; hostname verification on; NoTrustAllTest scans for trust-all code; harness adds a digest-pinned haproxy TLS proxy on 127.0.0.1 with a per-run key and IP SANs; waived: font-scale test for the prompt and an on-device user-CA test (JVM covers it)."
---

# Trust a self-signed certificate only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint after a disclaimer

## Problem

Users whose Tokkl server has a self-signed certificate cannot connect, because no system or user CA signed it. The PRD lets them trust that one certificate after reading a disclaimer that shows its SHA-256 fingerprint. The app must ask again when the certificate changes, and it must never accept a certificate without a check. The PRD puts this pin in a trust manager on the OkHttp client, separate from Android's trust store and the network security config.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:90
- docs/PRD.md:190
- docs/PRD.md:197
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

- When the TLS handshake fails on an untrusted chain, show a disclaimer with the leaf certificate's SHA-256 fingerprint and subject. The handshake fails before any HTTP request, so no credential has gone out yet. Capture the server's chain during the failed check so the prompt can show the leaf.
- On accept, store the fingerprint with the server configuration.
- Add a custom `X509TrustManager` to the shared client from TKA-0009. It first delegates to the platform trust manager, which covers system and user CAs. If that fails, it accepts only a leaf whose SHA-256 matches the stored pin. Otherwise it fails with a certificate-changed error that routes back to the prompt.
- Hostname verification stays on.
- No trust-all trust manager or hostname verifier exists anywhere in the codebase, including debug builds and tests.
- Keep the disclaimer text in English string resources and give every control a TalkBack label. Meet WCAG AA contrast, never use colour as the only signal, and keep it usable at the largest font scale. TKA-0022 shows a stronger warning for plain `http://`.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] An untrusted certificate shows the disclaimer with its SHA-256 fingerprint before any credential is sent.
- [ ] After the user accepts, connections succeed only with that certificate.
- [ ] A changed certificate fails and prompts again.
- [ ] A certificate trusted by a system or user CA never prompts.
- [ ] A JVM test shows a different certificate with the same subject is rejected.
- [ ] An instrumented test passes against the real Tokkl container behind a self-signed TLS proxy.
- [ ] No trust-all code path exists in the codebase.
- [ ] The disclaimer meets the string resource, TalkBack, contrast, colour and font scale rules.

---

# Keep the 429 sign-in cooldown across process death and recreation

## Problem

[credential-related line omitted]

## Expected behaviour

Within `Retry-After` of a 429, a recreated sign-in screen for the same server still shows the countdown and keeps Sign in disabled until the deadline passes.

## Steps to reproduce

[credential-related line omitted]
2. Within 60 seconds, kill the app process and open the app again.
3. Sign in is enabled and no countdown shows.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInModel.kt:57 creates `MutableStateFlow(SignInState())`, whose `retryAt` defaults to null (:34).
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/signin/SignInModel.kt:109 sets `retryAt` only in memory after a 429.
- Reported by the wave 4 code review run with `codex exec --yolo -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -`, severity minor, confidence high.

## Direction

- Persist the deadline on the phone keyed by server origin, with a wall-clock time that survives a reboot or an elapsed-realtime base that does not, and record the choice; restore it when the model starts and clear it once it passes or a sign-in succeeds.
- Store no credential with it, and keep it inside the existing backup exclusion.
- Never retry on the app's own; this only keeps the button disabled.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A test that recreates the model within the cooldown shows Sign in still disabled with the remaining seconds.
- [ ] After the deadline passes, or after a successful sign-in, the stored deadline is gone.
- [ ] Nothing besides the server origin and the deadline is stored for this.

id: TKA-0102
title: Show the chosen theme's background in the starting window, not the system's
status: done
type: bug
milestone: look-and-feel
priority: p3
effort: s
created: 2026-10-18
updated: 2026-10-18
labels: [theme, launch, settings]
cluster: tokkl-look
depends_on: [TKA-0015]
wave: 5
source: "sift-drain wave 4 sitting w4-a2, found while building TKA-0015"
resolution: "TKA-0102: on Android 12+ settings/ApplicationNightMode syncs UiModeManager.setApplicationNightMode with the stored mode (light NO, dark YES, system AUTO) on every start and change, off the main thread; MainActivity gets configChanges=uiMode so the sync does not recreate it; reproduced (15 sand frames with dark pinned on a light system) and fixed (navy only); below 12 unchanged (reasoned, no pre-12 AVD); waived: JVM test of the mode mapping (gate)."
---

# Show the chosen theme's background in the starting window, not the system's

## Problem

TKA-0015 lets the owner pin the app to light or dark. The app's own window follows that choice from its first frame, but Android draws the starting window from the manifest theme before the app runs. That theme takes its background from `res/values` or `res/values-night`, which match the system's mode. With the app pinned to dark on a light system, a cold start should show a sand starting window and then switch to navy, the flash that `WindowBackgroundTest` guards against when the app follows the system. Nobody has reproduced it on a device yet; the code paths below predict it.

## Expected behaviour

A cold start shows the chosen theme's background from the starting window on, in every theme mode, as `WindowBackgroundTest` checks for the follow-the-system mode.

## Steps to reproduce

1. On a phone in light mode, open Settings in the app and pick Dark.
2. Close the app from Recents, then start it from the launcher, with a screen recording running.
3. Look for sand frames before the first navy frame.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:20 gives the application `@style/Theme.Tokkl`, which Android uses for the starting window.
- app/src/main/res/values/themes.xml:10 and app/src/main/res/values-night/themes.xml:4 set `android:windowBackground` to `@color/window_background`, picked by the system's night mode.
- app/src/main/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/MainActivity.kt:59-62 repaints the window from the chosen scheme only once the activity composes.
- `grep -rn setApplicationNightMode app/src/main` finds nothing; only `WindowBackgroundTest` calls it.

## Direction

Reproduce first and record what the recording shows. On Android 12 and later, `UiModeManager.setApplicationNightMode` tells the system the app's night mode, which the starting window also uses: map light to `MODE_NIGHT_NO`, dark to `MODE_NIGHT_YES` and follow the system to `MODE_NIGHT_AUTO`. Call it when the setting changes and keep the stored setting as the source of truth. Check whether the call recreates the activity and whether the settings screen stays open when it does. `WindowBackgroundTest` sets the application night mode itself, so decide whether the app syncs it at start and adapt that test to drive the setting instead. Below Android 12 there is no such API; record whether that case stays as it is.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A recording or test shows whether the starting window flashes the system's colour with a pinned mode, before and after the change.
- [ ] On Android 12 and later, a cold start with a pinned light or dark mode shows that mode's background from the starting window on.
- [ ] Following the system still matches the system, and `WindowBackgroundTest` passes.

 If no device is attached, the script boots `tokkl-api36` as in the previous section, with 2 GB of guest RAM, and stops it and the adb server at the end. When your systemd user manager is running, the emulator runs in a scope of its own with `OOMPolicy=continue`. If the kernel's OOM killer ends qemu, the script fails and says so, and systemd leaves the session that started it alone. On an emulator it boots, the script also turns on `hide_error_dialogs` and closes open system dialogs, because a fresh headless boot often leaves "System UI isn't responding" in front of the app. It changes no setting on an emulator that was already running, and that emulator keeps running. +package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerConfigStore
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.ServerTrustStore
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.jsonPrimitive
+import org.junit.After
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.junit.Before
+import org.junit.Test
+
+/**
+ * The shared client against the real Tokkl container behind the TLS proxy
+ * that scripts/instrumented-tests.sh starts with a self-signed certificate.
+ * No system or user CA trusts that certificate, so only a pin lets a request
+ * through, and Android's own TLS stack runs the handshake.
+ */
+class PinnedCertificateTest {
+
+    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+    private val servers = ServerConfigStore(context)
+    private val trust = ServerTrustStore(context)
+    private val server = TokklTestServer.tlsUrl
+    private val me = server.api("me")
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+    @Before
+    fun configureTheServer() {
+        servers.save(server)
+        trust.clear()
+    }
+
+    @After
+    fun forgetTheServer() {
+        trust.clear()
+        servers.clear()
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun unpinnedCertificateFailsTheHandshakeWithItsFingerprint() {
+        val refusal = refusal(client.get(me, JsonObject.serializer()))
+
+        assertEquals(TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint, refusal.certificate.displaySha256)
+        assertEquals(me.host to me.port, refusal.certificate.host to refusal.certificate.port)
+        assertFalse(refusal.pinChanged)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun pinnedCertificateReachesTokkl() {
+        trust.pinCertificate(pin(TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint.replace(":", "").lowercase()))
+
+        val result = client.get(me, JsonObject.serializer())
+
+        val body = (result as? ApiResult.Success)?.value ?: throw AssertionError("GET /api/v9/me failed: ${(result as ApiResult.Failure).error.javaClass.simpleName}")
+        assertEquals(TokklTestServer.email, body.getValue("email").jsonPrimitive.content)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun anotherPinnedCertificateFailsAsAChangedCertificate() {
+        trust.pinCertificate(pin("00".repeat(32)))
+
+        val refusal = refusal(client.get(me, JsonObject.serializer()))
+
+        assertTrue(refusal.pinChanged)
+        assertEquals(TokklTestServer.tlsFingerprint, refusal.certificate.displaySha256)
+    }
+
+    private fun pin(sha256: String) = ServerCertificate(me.host, me.port, sha256, subject = "")
+
+    private fun refusal(result: ApiResult<*>): UntrustedCertificateException {
+        val error = (result as? ApiResult.Failure)?.error
+        val cause = (error as? ApiError.Network)?.cause ?: throw AssertionError("expected a network error, got ${error ?: "a success"}")
+        return generateSequence<Throwable>(cause) { it.cause }.filterIsInstance<UntrustedCertificateException>().firstOrNull()
+            ?: throw AssertionError("the handshake failed without the pinning trust manager's refusal: $cause")
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session
+
+import android.os.Process
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklApplication
+import java.io.File
+import java.time.DayOfWeek
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertNotEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.junit.FixMethodOrder
+import org.junit.Test
+import org.junit.runners.MethodSorters
+
+/**
+ * The account settings survive a restart of the app process, so screens have them offline. The
+ * steps run in name order, and the test orchestrator (app/build.gradle.kts) starts a new process
+ * for each. Each step reads what the step before stored through the app's own settings source,
+ * which sends no request. A step fails if it runs in the same process as the one before.
+ */
+@FixMethodOrder(MethodSorters.NAME_ASCENDING)
+class AccountSettingsRestartTest {
+
+    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+
+    private val source get() = (context.applicationContext as TokklApplication).accountSettings
+
+    /** Names the process that ran the step before. */
+    private val previousStep = File(context.cacheDir, "AccountSettingsRestartTest.process")
+
+    @Test
+    fun step1StoresSettingsWithATimeResolution() {
+        AccountSettingsStore(context).clear()
+        source.store(WITH_RESOLUTION)
+        previousStep.writeText(currentProcess())
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun step2KeepsThemAndStoresSettingsWithout() {
+        assertNewProcess()
+        assertEquals(WITH_RESOLUTION, source.state.value)
+        source.store(WITHOUT_RESOLUTION)
+        previousStep.writeText(currentProcess())
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun step3KeepsTheSettingsWithoutATimeResolution() {
+        try {
+            assertNewProcess()
+            assertEquals(WITHOUT_RESOLUTION, source.state.value)
+        } finally {
+            AccountSettingsStore(context).clear()
+            previousStep.delete()
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun assertNewProcess() {
+        assertTrue("the step before did not run", previousStep.exists())
+        assertNotEquals("the step before ran in this same process", previousStep.readText(), currentProcess())
+    }
+
+    /** A PID can be reused, so the process start time goes with it. */
+    private fun currentProcess(): String = "${Process.myPid()}@${Process.getStartElapsedRealtime()}"
+
+    private companion object {
+        val WITH_RESOLUTION = AccountSettings("Europe/Madrid", DayOfWeek.SUNDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = 15.0)
+        val WITHOUT_RESOLUTION = AccountSettings("Asia/Kolkata", DayOfWeek.SATURDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = null)
+    }
+}

new file mode 100644

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session
+
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.SessionRule
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklApplication
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.TokklTestServer
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiCredentials
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiError
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiResult
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.TogglApiClient
+import java.time.DayOfWeek
+import java.time.ZoneId
+import kotlinx.coroutines.runBlocking
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.buildJsonObject
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.put
+import org.junit.After
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Rule
+import org.junit.Test
+
+/**
+ * The app's own settings source reads the account settings from the real Tokkl container. The
+ * test changes the account's timezone and first day of the week, and puts back the UTC and
+ * Monday that scripts/instrumented-tests.sh set up.
+ */
+class AccountSettingsServerTest {
+
+    @get:Rule
+    val session = SessionRule()
+
+    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+    @After
+    fun restoreTheAccount() {
+        try {
+            putMe(timezone = "UTC", beginningOfWeek = 1)
+        } finally {
+            AccountSettingsStore(context).clear()
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun refreshReadsTheSettingsFromTheServerAndKeepsThem() {
+        // Tokkl keeps the name as sent. Intl accepts it in any case, and so does parseTimeZone.
+        putMe(timezone = "america/sao_paulo", beginningOfWeek = 0)
+        val source = (context.applicationContext as TokklApplication).accountSettings
+
+        runBlocking { source.refresh().join() }
+
+        // Tokkl's instance time resolution is 5 minutes unless an admin changes it.
+        val expected = AccountSettings("america/sao_paulo", DayOfWeek.SUNDAY, timeResolutionMinutes = 5.0)
+        assertEquals(expected, source.state.value)
+        assertEquals("the stored settings", expected, AccountSettingsStore(context).load())
+        assertEquals(ZoneId.of("America/Sao_Paulo"), expected.time()?.zone)
+    }
+
+    private fun putMe(timezone: String, beginningOfWeek: Int) {
+        val body = buildJsonObject {
+            put("timezone", timezone)
+            put("beginning_of_week", beginningOfWeek)
+        }
+        // Fails with the error alone, so neither a response body nor a secret lands in the message.
+        val result = client.send("PUT", client.server.api("me"), body, JsonObject.serializer())
+        if (result is ApiResult.Failure) {
+            val error = result.error

+        }

 fun expectedTheme(background: Color, dark: Boolean): ShownTheme =

+/**
+ * Whether [activity] runs in night mode. While the app follows the system, as every test starts,
+ * that is the system's mode. On Android 12 and later a light or dark setting replaces it.
+ */
 fun systemIsDark(activity: Activity): Boolean =
     (activity.resources.configuration.uiMode and Configuration.UI_MODE_NIGHT_MASK) == Configuration.UI_MODE_NIGHT_YES
 
+/**
+ * Waits until [activity] runs in the night mode Android gives the app for [mode]. On Android 12
+ * and later that is the chosen mode, and the configuration change arrives a moment after the
+ * choice. Below that it is the system's.
+ */
+fun ComposeTestRule.awaitNightModeFor(mode: ThemeMode, activity: Activity, systemDark: Boolean) {
+    val dark = if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.S) mode.isDark(systemDark) else systemDark
+    waitUntil("the activity runs in the night mode for $mode", 5_000) { systemIsDark(activity) == dark }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.signin
+
+import android.content.Context
+import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
+import org.junit.After
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Before
+import org.junit.Test
+
+/** The 429 deadlines on the phone hold each server origin and its deadline, and nothing else. */
+class SignInCooldownStoreTest {
+
+    private val context: Context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
+    private val store = SignInCooldownStore(context)
+
+    @Before
+    @After
+    fun forgetEverything() {
+        store.load().keys.forEach(store::clear)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun keepsOneDeadlinePerOriginUntilCleared() {
+        store.save("https://tokkl.example:443", 1_000L)
+        store.save("http://10.0.2.2:8080", 2_000L)
+        store.save("https://tokkl.example:443", 3_000L)
+
+        assertEquals(mapOf("https://tokkl.example:443" to 3_000L, "http://10.0.2.2:8080" to 2_000L), SignInCooldownStore(context).load())
+        val file = context.getSharedPreferences(SignInCooldownStore.FILE_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE).all
+        assertEquals("the file holds the origins and deadlines only", store.load(), file)
+
+        store.clear("https://tokkl.example:443")
+
+        assertEquals(mapOf("http://10.0.2.2:8080" to 2_000L), store.load())
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import android.annotation.SuppressLint
+import java.net.Socket
+import java.security.MessageDigest
+import java.security.cert.CertificateException
+import java.security.cert.X509Certificate
+import javax.net.ssl.SSLEngine
+import javax.net.ssl.SSLSocket
+import javax.net.ssl.X509ExtendedTrustManager
+import javax.net.ssl.X509TrustManager
+import javax.security.auth.x500.X500Principal
+
+/**
+ * A server's leaf certificate as the trust prompt shows it, and as a pin
+ * stores it: the TLS server's [host] and [port], the SHA-256 of the
+ * certificate's DER encoding as lowercase hex, and its subject. Public data
+ * only.
+ */
+data class ServerCertificate(val host: String, val port: Int, val sha256: String, val subject: String) {
+
+    /** The fingerprint as `AB:CD:…`, the form `openssl x509 -noout -fingerprint -sha256` prints. */
+    val displaySha256: String
+        get() = sha256.uppercase().chunked(2).joinToString(":")
+
+    companion object {
+        fun of(certificate: X509Certificate, host: String, port: Int): ServerCertificate =
+            ServerCertificate(
+                host = host,
+                port = port,
+                sha256 = sha256(certificate),
+                subject = certificate.subjectX500Principal.getName(X500Principal.RFC2253),
+            )
+
+        /** The SHA-256 of [certificate]'s DER encoding, as lowercase hex. */
+        fun sha256(certificate: X509Certificate): String =
+            MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256").digest(certificate.encoded).joinToString("") { "%02x".format(it) }
+    }
+}
+
+/**
+ * No system or user CA trusts the server's certificate, and the user has
+ * not pinned it. [pinChanged] is true when the user pinned another
+ * certificate for this host and port. The TLS handshake fails on this, before
+ * any HTTP request, so no credential has left the phone. The message names
+ * the host and port only.
+ */
+class UntrustedCertificateException(
+    val certificate: ServerCertificate,
+    val pinChanged: Boolean,
+    cause: CertificateException,
+) : CertificateException("No trusted CA or pin covers the certificate of ${certificate.host}:${certificate.port}", cause)
+
+/**
+ * The trust manager of [SharedHttpClient]. The [platform] trust manager
+ * decides first, so a certificate that a system or user CA signed is trusted
+ * as before and the network security config still applies. When the platform
+ * refuses the chain, the leaf is trusted only if its SHA-256 matches the pin
+ * the user stored for that host and port, read through [pinnedSha256].
+ * Anything else fails with [UntrustedCertificateException], which carries
+ * the leaf for the trust prompt.
+ *
+ * The pin covers the exact leaf certificate. The handshake proves the server
[credential-related line omitted]
+ * trusted. OkHttp still checks the host name against the leaf afterwards.
+ *
+ * Lint warns about any custom trust manager, since many skip the check. This
+ * one calls the platform's first and adds only the exact pinned leaf.
+ */
+@SuppressLint("CustomX509TrustManager")
+internal class PinningTrustManager(
+    private val platform: X509TrustManager,
+    private val pinnedSha256: (host: String, port: Int) -> String?,
+) : X509ExtendedTrustManager() {
+
+    override fun checkServerTrusted(chain: Array<X509Certificate>, authType: String, socket: Socket?) {
+        val session = (socket as? SSLSocket)?.handshakeSession
+        verify(chain, session?.peerHost, session?.peerPort ?: -1) {
+            if (platform is X509ExtendedTrustManager) {
+                platform.checkServerTrusted(chain, authType, socket)
+            } else {
+                platform.checkServerTrusted(chain, authType)
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    override fun checkServerTrusted(chain: Array<X509Certificate>, authType: String, engine: SSLEngine?) {
+        verify(chain, engine?.peerHost, engine?.peerPort ?: -1) {
+            if (platform is X509ExtendedTrustManager) {
+                platform.checkServerTrusted(chain, authType, engine)
+            } else {
+                platform.checkServerTrusted(chain, authType)
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** Without the server's host and port no pin applies, so the platform alone decides. */
+    override fun checkServerTrusted(chain: Array<X509Certificate>, authType: String) {
+        verify(chain, host = null, port = -1) { platform.checkServerTrusted(chain, authType) }
+    }
+
+    override fun checkClientTrusted(chain: Array<X509Certificate>, authType: String, socket: Socket?) {
+        throw CertificateException("The app does not accept client connections")
+    }
+
+    override fun checkClientTrusted(chain: Array<X509Certificate>, authType: String, engine: SSLEngine?) {
+        throw CertificateException("The app does not accept client connections")
+    }
+
+    override fun checkClientTrusted(chain: Array<X509Certificate>, authType: String) {
+        throw CertificateException("The app does not accept client connections")
+    }
+
+    override fun getAcceptedIssuers(): Array<X509Certificate> = platform.acceptedIssuers
+
+    /**
+     * Returns when [platformCheck] trusts [chain], or when its leaf matches the
+     * pin for [host] and [port]. Throws otherwise.
+     */
+    internal fun verify(chain: Array<out X509Certificate>, host: String?, port: Int, platformCheck: () -> Unit) {
+        val refusal = try {
+            platformCheck()
+            return
+        } catch (e: CertificateException) {
+            e
+        }
+        val leaf = chain.firstOrNull()
+        if (leaf == null || host == null || port !in 1..MAX_PORT) throw refusal
+        val seen = ServerCertificate.of(leaf, host, port)
+        val pin = pinnedSha256(host, port)
+        if (pin != null && MessageDigest.isEqual(pin.toByteArray(), seen.sha256.toByteArray())) return
+        throw UntrustedCertificateException(seen, pinChanged = pin != null, cause = refusal)
+    }
+
+    private companion object {
+        const val MAX_PORT = 65_535
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import java.io.IOException
+
+/**
+ * What the user has agreed to for a server: sending to a plain `http://` URL,
+ * and trusting a self-signed certificate by its fingerprint. [SharedHttpClient]
+ * checks it on every request and every TLS handshake, so no screen or
+ * background job can skip the question.
+ */
+interface ServerTrust {
+
+    /**
+     * Whether the user confirmed that requests to [server], a plain `http://`
[credential-related line omitted]
+     */
+    fun isCleartextConfirmed(server: ServerUrl): Boolean
+
+    /** Records the user's confirmation for [server]. Does disk I/O. */
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun confirmCleartext(server: ServerUrl)
+
+    /**
+     * The SHA-256 of the certificate the user trusted for the TLS server at
+     * [host] and [port], as lowercase hex, or null when there is none.
+     */
+    fun pinnedSha256(host: String, port: Int): String?
+
+    /** Trusts [certificate], and no other, for its host and port. Does disk I/O. */
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun pinCertificate(certificate: ServerCertificate)
+}
+
+/**
+ * Where [SharedHttpClient] reads the user's trust decisions from. The app sets
+ * this once at start. Until then nothing is trusted, so every request to a
+ * plain `http://` server is refused, and only certificates that a system or
+ * user CA signed pass.
+ */
+object ConfiguredTrust {
+
+    @Volatile
+    var current: ServerTrust = TrustNothing
+        private set
+
+    fun readFrom(trust: ServerTrust) {
+        current = trust
+    }
+
+    private object TrustNothing : ServerTrust {
+        override fun isCleartextConfirmed(server: ServerUrl): Boolean = false
+
+        override fun confirmCleartext(server: ServerUrl) {
+            throw IOException("No trust store is configured")
+        }
+
+        override fun pinnedSha256(host: String, port: Int): String? = null
+
+        override fun pinCertificate(certificate: ServerCertificate) {
+            throw IOException("No trust store is configured")
+        }
+    }
+}

+    /** `scheme://host:port`, with an IPv6 host in brackets. */
+    val origin: String

+
+    /** Whether requests to this server travel unencrypted, over plain `http://`. */
+    val isCleartext: Boolean
+        get() = !base.isHttps

     fun isSameOrigin(url: HttpUrl): Boolean =
         url.scheme == base.scheme && url.host == base.host && url.port == base.port

 }

+/** `scheme://host:port`, with an IPv6 host in brackets. Holds no path, query or credential. */
+internal fun HttpUrl.origin(): String {
+    val host = if (':' in host) "[$host]" else host
+    return "$scheme://$host:$port"
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session
+
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time.AccountTime
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time.parseTimeZone
+import java.time.Clock
+import java.time.DayOfWeek
+import kotlinx.serialization.SerialName
+import kotlinx.serialization.Serializable
+
+/**
+ * The settings from `GET /api/v9/me` that decide how the app shows and reads times (docs/PRD.md
+ * S7). They hold no credential.
+ *
+ * @property timeZone the account's timezone name, exactly as the server sent it.
+ * @property timeResolutionMinutes Tokkl's `time_resolution_minutes`, which the server snaps
+ *   stopped entries to. Null when `/me` leaves it out. Toggl Track and older Tokkl servers leave
+ *   it out, and they do not snap.
+ */
+data class AccountSettings(
+    val timeZone: String,
+    val beginningOfWeek: DayOfWeek,
+    val timeResolutionMinutes: Double?,
+) {
+
+    /**
+     * Times in the account's zone, read from [clock], or null when [parseTimeZone] cannot
+     * resolve [timeZone]. That happens when the server's Intl knows a zone that the phone's tz
+     * database lacks, such as a zone added after the phone's last tz update.
+     *
+     * The app never falls back to the device's zone or to UTC. A screen without an
+     * [AccountTime] shows no clock times or dates and takes no time input. Instead it says that
+     * the phone does not know the account's timezone, and names [timeZone]. Durations, the
+     * running clock, Start and Stop need no zone and keep working.
+     */
+    fun time(clock: Clock = Clock.systemUTC()): AccountTime? =
+        parseTimeZone(timeZone)?.let { zone -> AccountTime(zone, beginningOfWeek, timeResolutionMinutes, clock) }
+}
+
+/**
+ * The fields the app reads from `GET /api/v9/me`. Toggl Track and Tokkl always send `id`,
+ * `timezone` and `beginning_of_week`, so a body without them is not a `/me` user.
+ * `time_resolution_minutes` is Tokkl's own and may be missing. `toString` leaves out the token.
+ */
+@Serializable
+class Me(
+    val id: Long,
[credential-related line omitted]
+    val timezone: String,
+    @SerialName("beginning_of_week") val beginningOfWeek: Int,
+    @SerialName("time_resolution_minutes") val timeResolutionMinutes: Double? = null,
+) {
+
+    /** The account settings, or null when `beginning_of_week` is not a day from 0 to 6. */
+    fun accountSettings(): AccountSettings? =
+        dayOfWeek(beginningOfWeek)?.let { AccountSettings(timezone, it, timeResolutionMinutes) }
+
+    override fun toString(): String = "Me(id=$id)"
+}
+
+/** The day Toggl numbers [number], from 0 for Sunday to 6 for Saturday, or null outside that. */
+internal fun dayOfWeek(number: Int): DayOfWeek? =
+    if (number in 0..6) DayOfWeek.SUNDAY.plus(number.toLong()) else null
+
+/** Toggl's number for this day, from 0 for Sunday to 6 for Saturday. */
+internal val DayOfWeek.togglNumber: Int
+    get() = value % 7

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session
+
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ApiResult
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.TogglApiClient
+import java.io.IOException
+import kotlin.coroutines.CoroutineContext
+import kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineScope
+import kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineStart
+import kotlinx.coroutines.Job
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.MutableStateFlow
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.StateFlow
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.asStateFlow
+import kotlinx.coroutines.launch
+
+/**
+ * The account settings for the whole process, and the one place screens read them from.
+ * Sign-in stores the settings from its own `GET /api/v9/me`, and [refresh] reads them again, so
+ * a change made in the web app reaches the phone. They persist, so screens have them offline and
+ * after a restart. Screens take their zone, clock and week start from [AccountSettings.time],
+ * never from the device.
+ *
+ * @param initial the stored settings, or null when none are stored.
+ * @param save writes the settings, throwing [IOException] when it cannot.
+ * @param client the API client of the signed-in account, or null when signed out. Runs on [io].
+ * @param scope outlives every screen, so leaving the app does not cancel a refresh.
+ */
+class AccountSettingsSource(
+    initial: AccountSettings?,
+    private val save: (AccountSettings) -> Unit,
+    private val client: () -> TogglApiClient?,
+    private val scope: CoroutineScope,
+    private val io: CoroutineContext,
+) {
+
+    private val mutableState = MutableStateFlow(initial)
+
+    /** The stored settings, or null when none are stored yet. */
+    val state: StateFlow<AccountSettings?> = mutableState.asStateFlow()
+
+    /** Guards [writes], [refreshing], and each write together with the state it sets. */
+    private val lock = Any()
+
+    /** Counts the writes, so a refresh drops an answer that a newer write has replaced. */
+    private var writes = 0L
+
+    private var refreshing: Job? = null
+
+    /**
+     * Writes [settings] and shows them in [state]. Sign-in calls this with the settings from its
+     * own `GET /api/v9/me`. Blocks on disk I/O, so call it off the main thread.
+     */
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun store(settings: AccountSettings) {
+        synchronized(lock) { write(settings) }
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Reads `GET /api/v9/me` for the signed-in account and keeps its settings. Does nothing when
+     * signed out. A failed request or write keeps what is stored. While one refresh runs, another
+     * call returns that one instead of sending a second request.
+     */
+    fun refresh(): Job {
+        val job = synchronized(lock) {
+            refreshing?.takeUnless { it.isCompleted }
+                ?: scope.launch(io, start = CoroutineStart.LAZY) { refreshNow() }.also { refreshing = it }
+        }
+        job.start()
+        return job
+    }
+
+    private fun refreshNow() {
+        val writesBefore = synchronized(lock) { writes }
+        val api = client() ?: return
+        val me = api.get(api.server.api("me"), Me.serializer())
+        val settings = (me as? ApiResult.Success)?.value?.accountSettings() ?: return
+        synchronized(lock) {
+            // Sign-in stored newer settings while the request ran.
+            if (writes != writesBefore || settings == mutableState.value) return
+            try {
+                write(settings)
+            } catch (e: IOException) {
+                // Keeps the stored settings until the next refresh.
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** Only while holding [lock]. */
+    private fun write(settings: AccountSettings) {
+        save(settings)
+        writes++
+        mutableState.value = settings
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session
+
+import android.content.Context
+import android.content.SharedPreferences
+import java.io.IOException
+
+/**
+ * The account settings in their own private preferences file, so screens have them offline and
+ * after a restart. The file holds no credential. It stays on the phone, because the backup and
+ * data extraction rules leave out every domain.
+ *
+ * The first call loads the preferences file, so make calls off the main thread.
+ */
+class AccountSettingsStore(context: Context) {
+
+    private val preferences by lazy { context.getSharedPreferences(FILE_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE) }
+
+    /** The stored settings, or null when none are stored. */
+    fun load(): AccountSettings? {
+        val timeZone = preferences.getString(KEY_TIME_ZONE, null) ?: return null
+        val beginningOfWeek = dayOfWeek(preferences.getInt(KEY_BEGINNING_OF_WEEK, -1)) ?: return null
+        val timeResolution = preferences.getString(KEY_TIME_RESOLUTION_MINUTES, null)?.toDoubleOrNull()
+        return AccountSettings(timeZone, beginningOfWeek, timeResolution)
+    }
+
+    /** Replaces the stored settings in one write. */
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun save(settings: AccountSettings) {
+        commit {
+            putString(KEY_TIME_ZONE, settings.timeZone)
+            putInt(KEY_BEGINNING_OF_WEEK, settings.beginningOfWeek.togglNumber)
+            val timeResolution = settings.timeResolutionMinutes
+            if (timeResolution == null) {
+                remove(KEY_TIME_RESOLUTION_MINUTES)
+            } else {
+                // A Double's string form reads back as the same Double.
+                putString(KEY_TIME_RESOLUTION_MINUTES, timeResolution.toString())
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun clear() {
+        commit { clear() }
+    }
+
+    private fun commit(change: SharedPreferences.Editor.() -> Unit) {
+        if (!preferences.edit().apply(change).commit()) throw IOException("Could not write $FILE_NAME")
+    }
+
+    internal companion object {
+        const val FILE_NAME = "account_settings"
+        private const val KEY_TIME_ZONE = "timezone"
+        private const val KEY_BEGINNING_OF_WEEK = "beginning_of_week"
+        private const val KEY_TIME_RESOLUTION_MINUTES = "time_resolution_minutes"
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session
+
+import android.content.Context
+import android.content.SharedPreferences
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerCertificate
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerTrust
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerUrl
+import java.io.IOException
+
+/**
+ * The user's trust decisions, in the app's private preferences file
+ * `server_trust`, next to the server configuration: the one plain `http://`
+ * server URL the user confirmed, and the one pinned certificate, as the TLS
+ * server's host and port and the SHA-256 of its leaf certificate. A new
+ * confirmation or pin replaces the old one, so another URL or certificate
[credential-related line omitted]
+ * rules exclude it with every other file. Sign-out removes it with [clear].
+ *
+ * The first call loads the preferences file, so make calls off the main thread.
+ */
+class ServerTrustStore(context: Context) : ServerTrust {
+
+    private val preferences by lazy { context.getSharedPreferences(FILE_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE) }
+
+    override fun isCleartextConfirmed(server: ServerUrl): Boolean =
+        server.isCleartext && preferences.getString(KEY_CLEARTEXT_URL, null) == server.toString()
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    override fun confirmCleartext(server: ServerUrl) {
+        commit { putString(KEY_CLEARTEXT_URL, server.toString()) }
+    }
+
+    override fun pinnedSha256(host: String, port: Int): String? =
+        preferences.takeIf { it.getString(KEY_PIN_HOST, null) == host && it.getInt(KEY_PIN_PORT, -1) == port }
+            ?.getString(KEY_PIN_SHA256, null)
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    override fun pinCertificate(certificate: ServerCertificate) {
+        commit {
+            putString(KEY_PIN_HOST, certificate.host)
+            putInt(KEY_PIN_PORT, certificate.port)
+            putString(KEY_PIN_SHA256, certificate.sha256)
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** Forgets every decision, so each server asks again. */
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun clear() {
+        commit { clear() }
+    }
+
+    private fun commit(change: SharedPreferences.Editor.() -> Unit) {
+        if (!preferences.edit().apply(change).commit()) throw IOException("Could not write $FILE_NAME")
+    }
+
+    internal companion object {
+        const val FILE_NAME = "server_trust"
+        private const val KEY_CLEARTEXT_URL = "cleartext_url"
+        private const val KEY_PIN_HOST = "pin_host"
+        private const val KEY_PIN_PORT = "pin_port"
+        private const val KEY_PIN_SHA256 = "pin_sha256"
+    }
+}

PY" in /home/e0ipso/workspace/apps/tokkl-android--review-w5

otlinx.coroutines.CoroutineScope
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.Flow
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.distinctUntilChanged
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.flowOn
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.launchIn
+import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.onEach
+
+/** The night mode to give Android for this theme mode. Following the system leaves it to Android. */
+val ThemeMode.applicationNightMode: Int
+    get() = when (this) {
+        ThemeMode.SYSTEM -> UiModeManager.MODE_NIGHT_AUTO
+        ThemeMode.LIGHT -> UiModeManager.MODE_NIGHT_NO
+        ThemeMode.DARK -> UiModeManager.MODE_NIGHT_YES
+    }
+
+/**
+ * Gives Android each mode in [modes] as the app's night mode, starting with the current one.
+ * Android draws the starting window from the manifest theme before the app runs, and picks
+ * res/values or res/values-night by the night mode it keeps for the app. Without this, a light or
+ * dark setting that differs from the system's starts in the system's colours.
+ *
+ * The setting stays the source of truth, so this runs on every start too and repairs a night mode
+ * that went out of step. Each call is a binder call, so it runs on [io]. MainActivity handles the
+ * configuration change itself, so a change keeps the activity and the screen it shows.
+ *
+ * Android added the app's night mode in 12 (API 31). Below that the starting window follows the
+ * system's mode and this does nothing.
+ */
+fun syncApplicationNightMode(context: Context, modes: Flow<ThemeMode>, scope: CoroutineScope, io: CoroutineContext) {
+    if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT < Build.VERSION_CODES.S) return
+    val uiModeManager = context.getSystemService(UiModeManager::class.java)
+    modes.distinctUntilChanged()
+        .onEach { uiModeManager.setApplicationNightMode(it.applicationNightMode) }
+        .flowOn(io)
+        .launchIn(scope)
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.signin
+
+import android.content.Context
+import android.content.SharedPreferences
+import java.io.IOException
+
+/**
+ * The sign-in cooldowns in the app's private preferences file
+ * `sign_in_cooldown`: one entry per server origin, holding the wall-clock
+ * time at which that server's 429 stops blocking sign-in. The file holds
+ * nothing else, and the backup rules exclude it with every other file.
+ *
+ * The first call loads the preferences file, so make calls off the main thread.
+ */
+class SignInCooldownStore(context: Context) : SignInCooldowns {
+
+    private val preferences by lazy { context.getSharedPreferences(FILE_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE) }
+
+    override fun load(): Map<String, Long> =
+        preferences.all.mapNotNull { (origin, deadline) -> (deadline as? Long)?.let { origin to it } }.toMap()
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    override fun save(origin: String, deadline: Long) {
+        commit { putLong(origin, deadline) }
+    }
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    override fun clear(origin: String) {
+        commit { remove(origin) }
+    }
+
+    private fun commit(change: SharedPreferences.Editor.() -> Unit) {
+        if (!preferences.edit().apply(change).commit()) throw IOException("Could not write $FILE_NAME")
+    }
+
+    internal companion object {
+        const val FILE_NAME = "sign_in_cooldown"
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.signin
+
+import java.io.IOException
+
+/**
+ * Where the 429 deadlines of sign-in persist, by server origin
+ * (`scheme://host:port`), so a new process still waits them out. A deadline
+ * is a wall-clock time in milliseconds: Tokkl's lockout runs on the server's
+ * clock, so a reboot of the phone does not lift it. Holds nothing else. Calls
+ * do disk I/O.
+ */
+interface SignInCooldowns {
+
+    /** Every stored deadline, by origin. */
+    fun load(): Map<String, Long>
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun save(origin: String, deadline: Long)
+
+    @Throws(IOException::class)
+    fun clear(origin: String)
+}

+private fun CertificatePrompt(certificate: ServerCertificate, changed: Boolean, onTrust: () -> Unit, onCancel: () -> Unit) {

+    AlertDialog(
+        onDismissRequest = onCancel,
+        title = {
+            Text(

+                modifier = Modifier.semantics { heading() },
+            )
+        },
+        text = {
+            Column(
+                modifier = Modifier.verticalScroll(rememberScrollState()),
+                verticalArrangement = Arrangement.spacedBy(8.dp),
+            ) {
+                Text(stringResource(if (changed) R.string.sign_in_certificate_changed_body else R.string.sign_in_certificate_body, server))
+                Text(stringResource(R.string.sign_in_certificate_fingerprint), style = MaterialTheme.typography.titleSmall)
+                Text(certificate.displaySha256, fontFamily = FontFamily.Monospace)
+                Text(stringResource(R.string.sign_in_certificate_subject), style = MaterialTheme.typography.titleSmall)
+                Text(certificate.subject)
+            }
+        },
+        confirmButton = {
+            TextButton(onClick = onTrust) {
+                Text(stringResource(R.string.sign_in_certificate_trust))
+            }
+        },
+        dismissButton = {
+            TextButton(onClick = onCancel) {
+                Text(stringResource(R.string.sign_in_warning_cancel))
+            }
+        },
+    )
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+import java.time.Clock
+import java.time.DayOfWeek
+import java.time.Instant
+import java.time.LocalDate
+import java.time.ZoneId
+import java.time.ZonedDateTime
+import java.time.temporal.TemporalAdjusters
+
+/**
+ * Times in the account's timezone, the one zone the app shows and reads times in (docs/PRD.md
+ * S7). Screens take the zone, the current time, today's date and week boundaries from here and
+ * never from the device: no `ZoneId.systemDefault()`, no `TimeZone.getDefault()` and no `now()`
+ * without a clock. [com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.session.AccountSettings.time] builds one.
+ *
+ * Manual entry (TKA-0040) adds the conversion from a typed wall-clock time to an instant here,
+ * as a port of zonedToUtc in ../tokkl/packages/common/time.ts.
+ *
+ * @param clock where the current instant comes from. Its own zone does not matter.
+ */
+class AccountTime(
+    val zone: ZoneId,
+    val firstDayOfWeek: DayOfWeek,
+    private val timeResolutionMinutes: Double?,
+    clock: Clock = Clock.systemUTC(),
+) {
+
+    /** The clock in the account's zone, for java.time's `now(clock)` functions. */
+    val clock: Clock = clock.withZone(zone)
+
+    fun now(): ZonedDateTime = ZonedDateTime.now(clock)
+
+    fun today(): LocalDate = LocalDate.now(clock)
+
+    /** [instant] on the account's wall clock, for display. */
+    fun local(instant: Instant): ZonedDateTime = instant.atZone(zone)
+
+    /** The first day of the week that holds [date], as startOfWeek in ../tokkl/packages/common/time.ts. */
+    fun startOfWeek(date: LocalDate): LocalDate = date.with(TemporalAdjusters.previousOrSame(firstDayOfWeek))
+
+    /**
+

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+/** The user's trust decisions in memory, for tests. Starts with [confirmed] already confirmed. */
+class InMemoryServerTrust(vararg confirmed: ServerUrl) : ServerTrust {
+
+    val cleartextConfirmed: MutableSet<ServerUrl> = confirmed.toMutableSet()
+
+    /** Pinned SHA-256 fingerprints by `host:port`. */
+    val pins: MutableMap<String, String> = mutableMapOf()
+
+    override fun isCleartextConfirmed(server: ServerUrl): Boolean = server in cleartextConfirmed
+
+    override fun confirmCleartext(server: ServerUrl) {
+        cleartextConfirmed += server
+    }
+
+    override fun pinnedSha256(host: String, port: Int): String? = pins["$host:$port"]
+
+    override fun pinCertificate(certificate: ServerCertificate) {
+        pins["${certificate.host}:${certificate.port}"] = certificate.sha256
+    }
+}
